# Deduction for new investment assets — investment boost ¶10-945

#Note: The Taxation (Annual Rates for 2025–26, Compliance Simplification, and Remedial Measures) Bill proposes the following remedial amendments to the investment boost regime:

## to restore the $1,000 low-value asset threshold as this has been inadvertently raised to $1,250 for

asset eligible for a new investment asset deduction.

## to clarify a new asset transferee only inherits the new asset investment deduction when they

otherwise are treated as having taken the pre-transfer amounts of depreciation loss under another provision of the IT07.

## to confirm that when depreciable assets are sold between associated persons the purchaser's

depreciation loss is limited to the vendor's original cost and that cost is calculated before any new investment asset deduction claimed by the vendor.

## to clarify when an asset has never previously been used or available for use in New Zealand for the

purposes of the investment boost policy.

As part of the 2025 Budget, the coalition government introduced a new tax incentive where taxpayers can claim 20% deduction on expenditure incurred on a new investment asset. The objective of this rule is to encourage capital investment and raise productivity in New Zealand.

## Definition of "new investment assets"

A "new investment asset" is defined as an asset acquired and owned by a person in the following categories (s DI 4):

## all depreciable property for which the person is allowed a depreciation deduction, including

commercial buildings, but excluding residential dwelling and fixed life intangible property (FLIP)

## deductible improvements made by a person in relation to a farming business (s DO 4), aquacultural

business (s DO 12), or a forestry business (s DP 3)

## assets acquired as petroleum development expenditure and mining development expenditure but

excluding rights, permits or privileges

## deductible planting costs incurred by a person on planting listed horticultural plants.

For a new investment asset that is a depreciable property, the investment boost deduction may be claimed provided the asset first becomes available for use in New Zealand on or after 22 May 2025 (other than as trading stock). If the person already owns the asset, the investment boost deduction may be available provided the asset first becomes available for use on or after 22 May 2025.

For a new investment asset that is not a depreciable property, the deduction may be claimed provided the person incurs expenditure in relation to the asset on or after 22 May 2025.

## Calculation of the investment boost deduction

Under s DI 5, the investment boost deduction may be claimed in the income year an asset becomes a new investment asset is calculated using the following formula: 0.2 × (expenditure # contribution) where: expenditure is the expenditure incurred in acquiring the asset, excluding any expenditure incurred prior to 22 May 2025 on an asset that is not depreciable property

contribution is the total amount of capital contribution under s DB 64 and government grants under s DF 1(3). Section DI 5 overrides the capital limitation but other limitations still apply.

The amount of the deduction calculated under s DI 5 reduces the relevant adjusted tax value, base value, cost or value (as appropriate) for the purposes of calculating depreciation under the depreciation rules (s DI 6(a)). A person is able to claim both the investment boost deduction and depreciation in the year that the depreciable property first becomes available for use in New Zealand.

Note for depreciable property, the investment boost deduction may be recovered if the depreciable property is disposed of for more than its adjusted tax value (s EE 48(1B) and ¶13-300).

## Apportionment for mixed-use assets and changes to business-use portion

If a new investment asset is used for both business and private use, the deduction available under subpt DI is apportioned to reflect business use of the asset. If business use of an asset reduces by 25% or more in subsequent income years, assessable income may arise (s CC 15).

For practical examples illustrating the application of the investment boost rules, see Tax Information Bulletin ¶377-102 Vol 37, No 7, August 2025 at 4–7.

Law: IT07 ss CC 15, DI 1, DI 2, DI 3, DI 4, DI 5, DI 6

Last reviewed on 31 December 2025

What's New in NZ Updating Master Tax Guide

## December 2025

## NEW DEVELOPMENTS

## Purchase price allocation rules and sale of farmland

On 15 December 2025, Inland Revenue issued Questions We've Been Asked QB 25/22, "Do the purchase price allocation rules alter the tax book values of Farmland Improvements and listed horticultural plants under subpart DO?". This item confirms that the purchase price allocation rules do not override the tax treatment of farmland improvements and listed horticultural plants as specified under subpart DO. See ¶33-200.

## November 2025

## NEW DEVELOPMENTS

## GST – Meaning of payment

On 11 November 2025, Inland Revenue issued interpretation statement IS 25/23, "GST – Meaning of payment". This interpretation statement clarifies the Commissioner's view on the meaning of "payment" for GST purposes. The statement replaces "GST: Meaning of payment" Tax Information Bulletin ¶14-102 Vol 1, No 4 (October 1989): 1 and "GST: claims for secondhand goods input tax credits on property transactions between associated persons" Tax Information Bulletin ¶511-102 Vol 5, No 11 (April 1994): 1. See ¶32-025.

Income tax and GST – industries other than forestry registered in the Emissions Trading Scheme On 11 November 2025, Inland Revenue issued interpretation statement IS 25/24, "Income tax and GST – industries other than forestry registered in the Emissions Trading Scheme". This interpretation statement provides guidance on the income tax and GST treatment of NZUs for industries (other than the forestry industry which is taxed differently) registered in the Emissions Trading Scheme. See ¶5-228 and ¶32-034.

## October 2025

## NEW DEVELOPMENTS

## GST – taxable activity

On 8 October 2025, Inland Revenue issued interpretation statement IS 25/21, "GST – taxable activity".

This interpretation statement sets out the Commissioner's view on the meaning of 'taxable activity' under s 6 of the Goods and Services Tax Act 1985 and provides general guidance in determining GST obligations under s 8 and s 51. The statement replaces "GST on disposal of assets used principally in making exempt supplies", Tax Information Bulletin ¶17-109 Vol 1, No 7 (January 1990) and "Whether an activity is a GST taxable activity or a hobby", Tax Information Bulletin ¶614-104 Vol 6, No 14 (June 1995). See ¶32-024.

## September 2025

## NEW DEVELOPMENTS

## Student Loans – Overseas borrowers and their obligations

On 15 September 2025, Inland Revenue issued interpretation statement IS 25/20, "Student Loans – Overseas borrowers and their obligations". This interpretation statement outlines when a borrower will be a New Zealand based borrower and when they will be an overseas-based borrower. A borrower's

status as New Zealand or overseas based determines their repayment obligations and whether interest accrues on their loan. See ¶8-030.

Whether an off-market share cancellation is made in lieu of the payment of a dividend On 11 September 2025, Inland Revenue issued IS 25/19, "Income Tax – Whether an off-market share cancellation is made in lieu of the payment of a dividend". This interpretation statement discusses the factors to be taken into account in determining whether an off-market cancellation of shares is made in lieu of the payment of a dividend. See ¶16-705.

Operational position on the FBT treatment of open loop cards provided by employers On 8 September 2025, Inland Revenue released OP 25/02, "Commissioner's operational position on the FBT treatment of open loop cards provided by employers to employees". This operational position item outlines the Commissioner's view on the timing of taxpayers' implementation of the position outlined in QB 25/07, "What is the income tax treatment of gift cards and products provided as trade rebates or promotions?". See ¶21-430.

## August 2025

## NEW DEVELOPMENTS

## Tax obligations for payments made to board members

On 18 August 2025, Inland Revenue released GA 25/01, "Tax on any fees paid to a member of a board, committee, panel, review group or task force". This general article is prepared specifically in relation to the fees paid to a member of a board, committee, panel, review group or task force under the Cabinet Fees Framework published by the Cabinet Office. See ¶3-280.

Money and property received by New Zealand residents from overseas On 7 August 2025, Inland Revenue issued IS 25/18, "Income tax – Whether money or property received by New Zealand tax residents from overseas is income from a foreign trust". This interpretation statement discusses the tax treatment of amounts of money and property received by NZ residents from overseas, including by inheritance. See ¶25-360.

## July 2025

## NEW DEVELOPMENTS

Deductibility of expenses incurred on repairing a recently acquired capital asset On 11 July 2025, Inland Revenue issued Questions we've been asked, QB 25/17, "Income tax: Can I claim a deduction for expenses I incur on repairing a recently acquired capital asset?". This item considers whether a taxpayer can claim a deduction on the amount of expenditure incurred to repair a capital asset that they have recently acquired. See ¶10-840.

GST treatment for forfeited deposit issued On 15 July 2025, Inland Revenue released QB 25/18, "Does GST apply to a deposit the seller retains in a cancelled land sale agreement". This QWBA explains the GST implications when a land sale agreement is cancelled and the seller retains the deposit. See ¶32-520.

## June 2025

## NEW DEVELOPMENTS

## The Commissioner of Inland Revenue's search powers

On 27 June 2025, Inland Revenue released OS 25/04, "The Commissioner of Inland Revenue's search powers". This operational statement sets out the Commissioner's procedure when exercising their search power under ss 17, 17C and 17D of the Tax Administration Act and the Search and Surveillance Act 2012. See ¶1-590.

Section 17B notices On 26 June 2025, Inland Revenue released OS 25/05, "Section 17B Notices". This operational statement sets out the Commissioner's procedure when issuing notices under s 17B of the Tax Administration Act 1994. See ¶1-590.

## Determination for per diem allowances paid to contractors/entertainers issued

On 11 June 2025, Inland Revenue issued DET 25/03, "Determination of amount of a particular payment (being per diem allowances paid in the screen production industry". This determination applies to per diem allowances paid to contractors/entertainers working in the Screen Production Industry in New Zealand. See ¶3-457.

## May 2025

## NEW DEVELOPMENTS

## Tax residence and the government service rule

On 17 May 2025, Inland Revenue issued IS 25/17, "Tax residence – government service rule". This interpretation statement provides detailed guidance on how the government service rules applies and further discusses the articles of double tax agreements that may need to be considered. See ¶1-120.

Tax residence On 16 May 2025, Inland Revenue issued IS 25/16, "Tax residence". This interpretation statement explains the tax residence rules and covers the application of the rules for individuals, companies and trusts. See ¶1-120, ¶1-140 and ¶5-700.

Transfers of residential land between associated persons On 9 May 2025, Inland Revenue issued QB 25/15, "How do the bright-line rollover relief provisions apply to transfers of residential land between associated persons?". This question we've been asked item provides guidance on how the bright-line test and the rollover relief provisions apply to transfers of residential land between associated persons on or after 1 July 2024. See ¶7-545.

Bright-line test and the business premises exclusion On 9 May 2025, Inland Revenue issued QB 25/14, "When does the business premises exclusion to the bright-line test apply?". This question we've been asked item explains the business premises exclusion which applies for the purposes of the bright-line test. See ¶7-049.

Bright-line test and sale of lifestyle block On 9 May 2025, Inland Revenue issued QB 25/13, "When is the sale of a lifestyle block excluded from the bright-line test?". This question we've been asked item explains how to work out whether a lifestyle block sold within 2 years can be excluded from the bright-line test. See ¶7-215.

Bright-line test and sale of subdivided section On 9 May 2025, Inland Revenue issued QB 25/12, "How does the bright-line test apply to the sale of a subdivided section?". This question we've been asked item explains how to work out whether a section subdivided from residential land and sold within 2 years can be excluded from the bright-line test. See ¶7-215.

Bright-line start date for the 2-year bright-line test On 9 May 2025, Inland Revenue issued QB 25/11, "When is the bright-line start date for the 2-year bright-line test?". This question we've been asked item explains the bright-line start date for the purposes of working out whether residential land is potentially subject to the 2-year bright-line test. See ¶7-049.

Date of acquisition for the purposes of the land sale rules On 9 May 2025, Inland Revenue issued QB 25/10, "On what date is a person treated as acquiring land for the purposes of the land sale rules?". This question we've been asked item provides guidance on when a person acquires land for the purposes of the land sale rules. See ¶7-047.

Regular pattern of transactions On 9 May 2025, Inland Revenue issued QB 25/09, "When do I have a "regular pattern" of transactions that prevents me from using exclusions from the land sale rules for my residence or for my main home?". This question we've been asked item provides explanation on when someone is considered to have a "regular pattern" of transactions that prevents them from using the residential exclusion from s CB 6, as well as when someone will have a "regular pattern" of transactions that prevents them from using the main home exclusion from the 2-year bright-line test. See ¶7-211 and ¶7-215.

Property held in a close company rented out for short-stay accommodation On 23 May 2025, Inland Revenue released QB 25/16, "Income tax – How do the income tax rules apply when a close company provides short-stay accommodation?". This question we've been asked

sets out how the income tax rules apply if property held in a close company is rented out as short-stay accommodation. See ¶5-232 and ¶10-113.

Land acquired for a purpose or an intention of disposal On 9 May 2025, Inland Revenue released QB 25/08, "When is land acquired for a purpose or with an intention of disposal so that the amount derived from the sale is income?". This question we've been asked provides guidance on how s CB 6 applies and its relationship with the 2-year bright-line test .

See ¶7-045.

Arrangements involved tax losses carried forward On 4 May 2025, Inland Revenue released IS 25/14, "Income tax – arrangements involving tax losses carried forward under the business continuity rules". This interpretation statement sets out the Commissioner's view on the potential application of the specific anti-avoidance rules in ss GB 3BA, GB 3BAB and GB 3BAC when a company carries a tax loss forward under the business continuity rules.

See ¶18-035. 2025 CPI adjustment to standard-cost household service for childcare providers issued On 30 May 2025, Inland Revenue released CPI adjustment made to the standard-cost amounts for childcare service providers for the 2025 income year (DET 09/02 (CPI 2025)). The standard-cost of providing childcare in the person's home is $4.56 per hour per child. The fixed standard-cost for administration and record-keeping is $446 per annum. See ¶5-695. 2025 CPI adjustment to standard-cost household service for boarding service providers issued On 30 May 2025, Inland Revenue released CPI adjustment made to the standard-cost amounts for boarding service providers for the 2025 income year (DET 19/01 (CPI 2025)). The weekly standard- cost for the 2025 income year has been set at $237 per boarder per week. See ¶5-695. 2025 CPI adjustment to standard-cost household service for short-stay accommodation issued On 30 May 2025, Inland Revenue released CPI adjustments made to the standard-cost amounts for the 2025 income year (DET 19/02 (CPI 2025)). The standard costs are $63 per night if the host owns their home, and $57 per night if the host rents their home. See ¶5-695. 2025 CPI adjustment to square metre rate for dual use of premises issued On 30 May 2025, Inland Revenue released CPI adjustments made to the square metre rate for the 2025 income year (OS 19/03 (CPI 2025)) and this is set at $55.60. See ¶10-530. 2025 CPI adjustment to kilometre rates for business use of vehicle issued On 30 May 2025, Inland Revenue released CPI adjustments made to the kilometre rates for the 2025 income year (OS 19/04 (KM 2025)). See ¶3-445, ¶10-710 and ¶50-084.

Specified Livestock Determination 2025 (NAMV 2025) issued On 26 May 2025 Inland Revenue released the National Average Market Values of Specified Livestock Determination 2025 (NAMV 2025) which declares the national average market values for the 2025 income year and applies to specified livestock on hand at the end of the 2024-2025 income year. See ¶27-264.

## April 2025

## NEW DEVELOPMENTS

## Access to a client's Inland Revenue information

On 17 April 2025, Inland Revenue issued OS 25/03, "Authority to Act for Tax Agents, Representatives and Nominated Persons: Access to a Client's Inland Revenue Information". This operational statement sets out how a tax agent, or a representative can obtain the authority to act from their clients and the process for a person to get another person to act for them in relation to their tax affairs and/or their social policy entitlements and obligations. See ¶2-018.

Moving between the standard tax rules and the mixed-use asset rules On 4 April 2025, Inland Revenue issued IS 25/08, "Income tax – implications of a residential property moving between the standard tax rules and the mixed-use asset rules". This interpretation statement provides guidance when a person's use of their residential property has changed so the property moves from being under one set of income tax deduction rules to another. The item explains how a person determines which income tax deduction rules apply and the consequences of moving between the standard tax rules and the mixed-use asset rules. See ¶10-037.

## Property held in a trust rented out for short-stay accommodation

On 3 April 2025, Inland Revenue released QB 25/05, "Income tax – If property held in a trust is rented out for short-stay accommodation, who declares the income and what deductions can be claimed?".

This question we've been asked sets out how the income tax rules apply if property held in a trust is rented out as short-stay accommodation. See ¶5-232 and ¶25-285.

Application of the standard tax rules for property used to provide short-stay accommodation On 3 April 2025, Inland Revenue released QB 25/04, "Income tax – How do the standard tax rules apply if I provide short-stay accommodation?". This question we've been asked explains how the standard tax rules apply to a dwelling that a taxpayer sometimes rent out as short-stay accommodation and also sometimes use privately (such as a holiday home). See ¶5-232 and ¶10-113.

Application of the mixed-use asset rules for property used to provide short-stay accommodation On 3 April 2025, Inland Revenue released QB 25/03, "Income tax – How do the mixed-use asset rules apply if I provide short-stay accommodation?". This question we've been asked sets out how the mixed-use asset rules apply to a dwelling that a taxpayer sometimes rent out as short-stay accommodation and also sometimes use privately (such as a holiday home). See ¶5-232, ¶10-035 and ¶10-113.

Which income tax rules apply to a dwelling sometimes rented out as short-stay accommodation and sometimes used privately On 3 April 2025, Inland Revenue issued QB 25/02, "Income tax – Which rules apply if I have a dwelling I sometimes rent out as short-stay accommodation and also sometimes use privately?". This question we've been asked explains which income tax rules apply to a dwelling that a taxpayer sometimes rent out as short-stay accommodation and also sometimes use privately (such as a holiday home). See ¶5-232, ¶10-035 and ¶10-113.

Taxpayer's own home or a separate dwelling on the property occasionally rented out as short- stay accommodation On 3 April 2025, Inland Revenue released QB 25/01, "Income tax – Which rules apply if I rent out my home, part of my home, or a separate dwelling on my property as short-stay accommodation?". This question we've been asked sets out how the income tax rules apply if a taxpayer occasionally rent out their home, a room in their home, or a separate dwelling on the property for short stays. See ¶5-232 and ¶10-113.

Income tax and GST consequences for forestry activities in the Emission Trading Scheme On 7 April 2025, Inland Revenue issued IS 25/13, "Income Tax and GST – forestry activities registered in the Emissions Trading Scheme". This interpretation statement sets out the income tax and GST treatment for forestry activities registered in the Emission Trading Scheme including the tax consequences of receiving, selling and surrendering emissions units (NZUs) and specific transactions involving NZUs. See ¶5-228 and ¶32-034.

Using the cost method to calculate FIF income On 4 April 2025, Inland Revenue issued IS 25/12, "Income tax – Using the cost method to determine foreign investment fund (FIF) income". This interpretation statement sets out when a New Zealand tax resident investor is able to apply the cost method to calculate their FIF income on shares held in foreign companies. See ¶26-130.

Tax treatment of losses on amalgamation On 4 April 2025, Inland Revenue released IS 25/09, "Tax treatment of losses on amalgamation". This interpretation statement provides guidance on how the provisions in subpart IE apply to the tax losses of an amalgamated company, amalgamating company or a non-amalgamating group company, that arise before amalgamation. See ¶20-120.

Calculating ASC for amalgamated companies On 4 April 2025, Inland Revenue issued QB 25/06, "How does an amalgamated company calculate its available subscribed capital following an amalgamation?". This question we've been asked item provides detailed guidance on how an amalgamated company calculates its available subscribed capital. See ¶16-760.

Consultation paper on proposals for fringe benefit tax On 1 April 2025, Inland Revenue released the consultation paper, "Fringe benefit tax – options for change". This paper proposes a number of significant changes to the FBT regime with the aim to

simplify the FBT rules and reduce compliance costs for taxpayers. Submissions on the paper close on 5 May 2025 and the final proposals are expected to be included in a future tax bill. See ¶21-010.

Questions We've Been Asked on gift cards and product provided to employees On 16 April 2025, Inland Revenue released QB 25/07, "What is the income tax treatment of gift cards and products provided as trade rebates or promotions?", which explains the income tax treatment for trade rebate arrangements between businesses where trade suppliers provide products or gift cards to their trade customers. The provision of these products or gift cards by a business to its employees may give rise to employment income, a fringe benefit or a dividend in the case of shareholder-employees.

See ¶21-430.

## March 2025

## NEW DEVELOPMENTS

## Cash collateral is "money lent"

On 18 March 2025, Inland Revenue issued OS 25/01, "Cash collateral is "money lent"". This operational statement explains a change in the Commissioner's view that interest arising on cash collateral is subject to resident withholding tax (RWT) or non-resident withholding tax (NRWT). See ¶10-182.

Interpretation statement on employer obligations for employee share scheme benefits paid in shares On 31 March 2025, Inland Revenue issued IS 25/07, "Employer obligations for employee share scheme benefits paid in cash". This interpretation statement provides guidance on an employer's withholding and reporting obligations when an employer chooses to fund the tax cost on an employee share scheme benefit provided in shares. See ¶3-470.

Interpretation statement on what is an employee share scheme, the taxing date and apportionment On 31 March 2025, Inland Revenue issued IS 25/06, "Employer obligations for employee share scheme benefits paid in cash". This interpretation statement provides guidance on an employers' PAYE, student loan and KiwiSaver obligations when an employee receives a benefit under an ESS that is paid in cash. See ¶3-470.

Interpretation statement on what is an employee share scheme, the taxing date and apportionment On 10 March 2025, Inland Revenue issued IS 25/04, "What an employee share scheme is, the taxing date and apportionment". This interpretation statement considers the definition of an employee share scheme, when the share scheme taxing date arises and when shares are held by or for the benefit of an employee, as well as the circumstances when the share scheme taxing date may be deferred.

The statement also addresses how benefits are apportioned when some of the employee's entitlement arises for non-residents. See ¶3-470. 2025 International tax disclosure exemption issued On 31 March 2025, Inland Revenue issued the 2025 International tax disclosure exemption determination (ITR 36). This determination sets out the disclosure exemption for disclosing FIF and CFC interests for the income year ended 31 March 2025. See ¶26-042 and ¶26-110.

GST treatment of fees paid in relation to managed fund On 31 March 2025, Inland Revenue released IS 25/05, "GST treatment of fees paid in relation to managed funds". This interpretation statement addresses the GST treatment of fees received by a manager of a managed fund and fees received by third-party suppliers for supplies made to the manager of a managed fund. See ¶32-044.

Commissioner's operational position on the GST treatment of fees paid in relation to managed fund On 31 March 2025, Inland Revenue issued OP 25/01, "Commissioner's operational position on the GST treatment of fees paid in relation to managed fund". This statement outlines the Commissioner's operational position on the adoption of IS 25/05. See ¶32-044.

GST on supplies through electronic marketplaces – hostel and motel opt-out agreement criteria

On 14 March 2025, Inland Revenue released DET 25/01, "GST on supplies through electronic marketplaces – hostel and motel opt-out agreement criteria". This determination sets out criteria for when a person who supplies accommodation through an electronic marketplace can enter into an opt- out agreement with the operator of an electronic marketplace. See ¶32-640.

## February 2025

## NEW DEVELOPMENTS

## National standard costs for specified livestock determination issued

On 24 February 2025 Inland Revenue issued "National standard costs for specified livestock determination 2025". This determination applies to any specified livestock on hand at the end of the 2024-2025 income year where the taxpayer has elected to value that livestock under the national standard cost scheme for that income year. See ¶27-267.

## January 2025

## NEW DEVELOPMENTS

## Identification of the relevant item of depreciable property

On 22 January 2025, Inland Revenue issued IS 25/03, "Income tax - identifying the relevant item of property for depreciation purposes". This interpretation statement provides guidance on how to identify the relevant item of property when applying the depreciation rules. See ¶13-034.

Interpretation statement on FBT implications for travel by motor vehicle between work and home On 15 January 2025 Inland Revenue released IS 25/02, "FBT – travel by motor vehicle between home and work", which considers when employer provided travel by motor vehicle is a fringe benefit and subject to FBT for travel between work and home. See ¶21-175.

Interpretation statement on deductibility of travel by motor vehicle between work and home On 15 January 2025 Inland Revenue released IS 25/01, "Income Tax – deducting costs of travel by motor vehicle between home and work", which considers who can claim deductions for expenditure on travel by motor vehicle under specific 4deductibility rules for motor vehicle expenditure, and in what circumstances. See ¶10-710.

Determination on amoritsation rates for M#nuka plants On 6 January 2025, Inland Revenue issued DET 24/04, "Amortisation rates for listed horticultural plants". This supplementary determination acknowledges that M#nuka, cultivated and managed as part of a farming activity primarily to promote the production of honey or another m#nuka product (not being timber), is recognised by the Commissioner as a listed horticultural plant for tax purposes. The Commissioner sets out the amortisation rates for M#nuka plants in this supplementary determination and states that this determination applies from the 2024 income year. See ¶50-167.

Introduction to Case Table

This table lists and locates all cases referred to in the New Zealand Master Tax Guide. References are to paragraph (¶) numbers.

Cases involving the Commissioner of Inland Revenue or other taxation authorities are listed alphabetically according to the name of the taxpayer. Cases decided by a Taxation Review Authority or Taxation Board of Review are listed alphabetically under "Taxation Review Authority" or "Taxation Board of Review" respectively.

## 2025 — The year at a glance [¶1]

[¶1]

## LEGISLATION

Taxation (Annual Rates for 2024–25, Emergency Response, and Remedial Measures) Act 2025 Taxation (Annual Rates for 2024–25, Emergency Response, and Remedial Measures) Act 2025 received the Royal assent on 29 March 2025.

Some of the main changes made by the Act include:

## confirming the annual income tax rates for 2024–25 income year — see CCH, New Zealand Tax

Rates, Dates and Depreciation Rates 2026 (CCH, Dep Rates 2026)

## introducing a generic set of tax relief for future emergency events, including:

## tax relief for employer's welfare contribution to employees (¶5-715)

## fringe benefit tax (FBT) exemption for a benefit provided by an employer to an employee after an

emergency event (¶21-665)

## allowing the Commissioner to remit use of money interest (UOMI) following the declaration of an

emergency event (¶14-180)

## tax relief where access to depreciable property is denied due to an emergency event (¶13-044)

## option to defer the recognition of depreciation recovery income in relation to depreciable property

affected by an emergency event (¶13-300)

## depreciation rollover relief for assets that are assessed as being uneconomic to repair due to an

emergency event (¶13-300)

## optional timing rule for assets damaged due to an emergency event but repairable (¶13-300)

## cap on depreciation recovery income where insurance received for repairable assets (¶13-300)

## optional adjustment to assets under thin-capitalisation rules after an emergency event (¶13-300)

## rollover relief for land and building held on revenue account and turning off bright-line and other

timing land disposal tests (¶7-451)

## implementing the OECD's Crypto-Asset Reporting Framework (¶26-280)

## increasing the exempt employee share scheme (ESS) thresholds (¶3-471)

## allowing certain borrowers to retrospectively register a security for Approved Issuer Levy (AIL) in

limited circumstances (¶30-320)

## introducing a scheme pays option for transfers of overseas pension funds to certain New Zealand

superannuation schemes (¶29-200)

## clarifying the transfer pricing rules and the dividend rules apply concurrently (¶26-608)

## extending the annual application due date for Research and Development Tax Incentive (RDTI)

general approval applications (¶11-150)

## introducing a number of partnership remedials, including:

## clarifying that a limited partnership is treated as a company for the purposes of the associated

persons tests in certain situations and the application of the limited partnership aggregation rules (¶16-245)

## allowing certain limited partnerships to access the AIL regime (¶30-310)

## allowing limited partnerships to apply for resident withholding tax (RWT)-exempt status at the

partnership level (¶15-160)

## allowing partners who are part of a partnership with a non-standard balance date to include their

share of the partnership income in the same corresponding income year as the partnership when filing their separate return (¶23-100)

## clarifying that the look-through company aggregation rules apply so that a person who is associated

with an owner of an effective look-through interest is treated as being associated with a look-through company (¶16-245)

## introducing an option for certain suppliers of listed services to include flat-rate credit as assessable

income (¶32-640)

## introducing an optional accounting rule for operators of electronic marketplaces, listing intermediaries

and underlying suppliers to account for output tax on a supply of taxable accommodation (¶32-640)

## introducing an amendment to limit input tax deductions on arrangements when secondhand goods

have been sold between several associated person before being sold by an unregistered supplier to an associated registered person (¶32-686)

## removing the requirement for a financial services supplier to notify Inland Revenue (IR) of their

election to zero-rate business-to-business supplies of financial services (¶32-310)

## clarifying that income subject to the minor or corporate beneficiary rules is subject to a 39% tax rate

(¶25-080).

Taxation (Budget Measures) Act 2025 The Taxation (Budget Measures) Act 2025 received the Royal assent on 29 May 2025.

The changes made by the Act include:

## introducing the new Investment Boost deduction for qualifying new investment assets (¶10-945)

## changing the KiwiSaver scheme settings, including:

## allowing a person aged under 16 to enroll in KiwiSaver in limited circumstances from 1 July 2025

(¶29-215)

## decreasing the age of entitlement for the government contribution to 16 years of age from 1 July 2025

(¶29-215)

## decreasing the government contribution to a maximum of $260.72 per year and this is not available to

members earning over $180,000 from 1 July 2025 (¶29-250)

## increasing the default rate of employee and employer KiwiSaver contribution from 3% to 3.5% from 1

April 2026 unless the employee applies for a temporary rate reduction (¶29-230)

## increasing the default rate of employee and employer KiwiSaver contribution to 4% from 1 April 2028

unless the employee applies for a temporary rate reduction (¶29-230)

## extending the income test to the first year of the Best Start tax credits (¶12-165)

## increasing the Working for Families abatement threshold from $42,700 to $44,900 and increasing the

Working for Families abatement rate from 27% to 27.5% from 1 April 2026 (¶12-150 and ¶50-041).

Income Tax (FamilyBoost) Amendment Act 2025 The Income Tax (FamilyBoost) Amendment Act 2025 received the Royal assent on 23 September 2025. The Act increases the percentage of early childhood education expenses claimable from 25% to 40%, raises the maximum quarterly payment from $975 to $1,560 and increases the maximum qualifying household income per quarter from $45,000 to $57,286 from 1 July 2025 (¶11-200).

Orders in Council Below are some of the key orders made in 2025: Tax Administration (FamilyBoost Tax Credit—Extension of Dates to File Return of Income) Order

The Tax Administration (FamilyBoost Tax Credit—Extension of Dates to File Return of Income) Order 2025 came into force on 26 February 2025 and allows FamilyBoost applicants who filed tax returns late, up to 26 February 2025, to access both FamilyBoost quarterly payments, once they have filed the tax return required for the quarter they are applying for. If a person to whom this order applies filed their last return of income after the date required by s MH 3(4) of the Income Tax Act 2007, and that date was before this order comes into force, the date is extended to 31 March 2025 for the purpose of the application for a refund. In practical terms, this order applies to:

- returns of income for the 2023–24 tax year that were filed late

- returns of income for the 2022–23 tax year that were filed late (if the person's 2023–24 return has not

  been filed, and is not late for filing).

Tax Administration (Reportable Jurisdictions for Application of CRS Standard) Amendment Regulations 2025 The Tax Administration (Reportable Jurisdictions for Application of CRS Standard) Amendment Regulations 2025 came into force on 31 March 2025 and adds Armenia, Jordan, Rwanda, Senegal, and Tunisia to New Zealand's existing list of 106 reportable jurisdictions under the Common Reporting Standard.

Income Tax (Fringe Benefit Tax, Interest on Loans) Amendment Regulations 2025 The Income Tax (Fringe Benefit Tax, Interest on Loans) Amendment Regulations 2025 decreases the FBT prescribed rate of interest for low-interest employment-related loans from 8.41% to 7.38% for the quarter beginning 1 April 2025. See CCH, Dep Rates 2026, ¶50-082.

Income Tax (Fringe Benefit Tax, Interest on Loans) Amendment Regulations (No 2) 2025 The Income Tax (Fringe Benefit Tax, Interest on Loans) Amendment Regulations (No 2) 2025 decreases the FBT prescribed rate of interest for low-interest employment-related loans from 7.38% to 6.67% for the quarter beginning 1 July 2025. See CCH, Dep Rates 2026, ¶50-082.

Income Tax (Fringe Benefit Tax, Interest on Loans) Amendment Regulations (No 3) 2025 The Income Tax (Fringe Benefit Tax, Interest on Loans) Amendment Regulations (No 3) 2025 decreases the FBT prescribed rate of interest for low-interest employment-related loans from 6.67% to 6.29% for the quarter beginning 1 October 2025. See CCH, Dep Rates 2026, ¶50-082.

Taxation (Use of Money Interest Rates) Amendment Regulations 2025 The Taxation (Use of Money Interest Rates) Amendment Regulations 2025 decreases the UOMI rates from 8 May 2025. See CCH, Dep Rates 2026, ¶50-083.

Taxation (Use of Money Interest Rates) Amendment Regulations (No 2) 2025 The Taxation (Use of Money Interest Rates) Amendment Regulations (No 2) 2025 decreases the UOMI rates from 16 January 2026. See CCH, Dep Rates 2026, ¶50-083.

Income Tax (Deemed Rate of Return on Attributing Interests in Foreign Investment Funds, 2024–25 Income Year) Order 2025 The Income Tax (Deemed Rate of Return on Attributing Interests in Foreign Investment Funds, 2024–25 Income Year) Order 2025 came into force on 25 July 2025 and sets the deemed rate of return at 8.04% for calculating foreign investment fund (FIF) income for the 2024–25 income year under the deemed rate of return calculation method. See ¶26-130.

Tax Administration (Extension of Application Deadline for Research and Development Tax Credits) Order 2025 The Tax Administration (Extension of Application Deadline for Research and Development Tax Credits) Order 2025 comes into force on 18 December 2025 and extends the due date for RDTI general approval for businesses with a September balance date for the 2024–25 income year to 15 January 2026. See ¶11-150.

Income Tax (Tax Credit) Order 2025 The Income Tax (Tax Credit) Order 2025 comes into force on 1 April 2026. It increases the Best Start Tax Credit rate from $3,838 to $4,041 and the minimum family tax credit threshold from $35,316 to $36,604. See ¶12-160 and ¶12-165.

## INTERPRETATION STATEMENTS

IR released the following interpretation statements in 2025:

IS 25/01

IS 25/01, "Income Tax — deducting costs of travel by motor vehicle between home and work", considers who can claim deductions for expenditure on travel by motor vehicle under specific deductibility rules for motor vehicle expenditure, and in what circumstances. It replaces IS3448. See ¶10-710.

## IS 25/02

IS 25/02, "FBT — travel by motor vehicle between home and work", considers when employer provided travel by motor vehicle is a fringe benefit and subject to FBT for travel between work and home. It replaces IS3448. See ¶21-175.

## IS 25/03

IS 25/03, "Income tax — identifying the relevant item of property for depreciation purposes", provides guidance on how to identify the relevant item of property when applying the depreciation rules. See ¶13-034.

## IS 25/04

IS 25/04, "What an employee share scheme is, the taxing date and apportionment", explains the definition of ESS, the relevant exclusions, when the share scheme taxing date arises and when shares are held by or for the benefit of an employee. See ¶3-470.

## IS 25/05

IS 25/05, "GST treatment of fees paid in relation to managed funds", addresses the GST treatment of fees received by a manager of a managed fund and fees received by third-party suppliers for supplies made to the manager of a managed fund. See ¶32-044.

## IS 25/06

IS 25/06, "Employer obligations for employee share scheme benefits paid in cash", provides guidance on an employers' PAYE, student loan and KiwiSaver obligations when an employee receives a benefit under an ESS that is paid in cash. See ¶3-470.

## IS 25/07

IS 25/07, "Employer obligations for employee share scheme benefits paid in cash". This interpretation statement provides guidance on an employer's withholding and reporting obligations when an employer chooses to fund the tax cost on an ESS benefit provided in shares. See ¶3-470.

## IS 25/08

IS 25/08, "Income tax — implications of a residential property moving between the standard tax rules and the mixed-use asset rules", provides guidance when a person's use of their residential property has changed so the property moves from being under one set of income tax deduction rules to another. See ¶10-037.

## IS 25/09

IS 25/09, "Tax treatment of losses on amalgamation", provides guidance on how the provisions in subpart IE apply to the tax losses of an amalgamated company, amalgamating company or a non-amalgamating group company, that arise before amalgamation. See ¶20-120.

## IS 25/10

IS 25/10, "Income tax and GST — Amalgamations", provides detailed guidance on the income tax and GST treatment of company amalgamations. See ¶20-090 and ¶20-135.

IS 25/11

IS 25/11, "Income tax — Partnerships (including limited partnerships) — general guidance", provides guidance on the income tax treatment of partnerships. See ¶23-010.

## IS 25/12

IS 25/12, "Income tax — Using the cost method to determine foreign investment fund (FIF) income", explains when a New Zealand tax resident investor is able to apply the cost method to calculate their FIF income on shares held in foreign companies. See ¶26-130.

## IS 25/13

IS 25/13, "Income Tax and GST — forestry activities registered in the Emissions Trading Scheme", sets out the income tax and GST treatment for forestry activities registered in the Emission Trading Scheme including the tax consequences of receiving, selling and surrendering emissions units (NZUs) and specific transactions involving NZUs. See ¶5-228 and ¶32-034.

## IS 25/14

IS 25/14, "Income tax — arrangements involving tax losses carried forward under the business continuity rules", sets out the Commissioner's view on the potential application of the specific anti-avoidance rules in ss GB 3BA, GB 3BAB and GB 3BAC when a company carries a tax loss forward under the business continuity rules. See ¶18-035.

## IS 25/16

IS 25/16, "Tax residence", explains the tax residence rules and covers the application of the rules for individuals, companies and trusts. See ¶1-120, ¶1-140 and ¶5-700.

## IS 25/17

IS 25/17, "Tax residence – government service rule", provides detailed guidance on how the government service rules applies and further discusses the articles of double tax agreements that may need to be considered. See ¶1-120.

## IS 25/18

IS 25/18, "Income tax — Whether money or property received by New Zealand tax residents from overseas is income from a foreign trust", discusses the tax treatment of amounts of money and property received by New Zealand residents from overseas, including by inheritance. See ¶25-360.

## IS 25/19

IS 25/19, "Income Tax — Whether an off-market share cancellation is made in lieu of the payment of a dividend", discusses the factors to be taken into account in determining whether an off-market cancellation of shares is made in lieu of the payment of a dividend. See ¶16-705.

## IS 25/20

IS 25/20, "Student Loans — Overseas borrowers and their obligations", outlines when a borrower will be a New Zealand based borrower and when they will be an overseas-based borrower. See ¶8-030.

## IS 25/21

IS 25/21, "GST — taxable activity", sets out the Commissioner's view on the meaning of 'taxable activity' under s 6 of the Goods and Services Tax Act 1985 and provides general guidance in determining GST obligations under ss 8 and 51. See ¶32-024.

## IS 25/22

IS 25/22, "GST — Secondhand goods input tax deduction", explains the requirements that must be met for a registered person to claim a secondhand goods input tax deduction. See ¶32-686.

## IS 25/23

IS 25/23, "GST — Meaning of payment", clarifies the Commissioner's view on the meaning of "payment" for GST purposes. See ¶32-025.

## IS 25/24

IS 25/24, "Income tax and GST — industries other than forestry registered in the Emissions Trading Scheme", provides guidance on the income tax and GST treatment of NZUs for industries (other than the forestry industry which is taxed differently) registered in the Emissions Trading Scheme. See ¶5-228 and ¶32-034.

## IS 25/25

IS 25/25, "Income tax — business activity", explains whether and when a taxpayer is carrying on a "business" for income tax purposes. See ¶5-172.

## IS 25/26

IS 25/26, "The Commissioner's duty of care and management — section 6A of the Tax Administration Act 1994" clarifies the Commissioner's view on his "care and management" duty under s 6A of the Tax Administration Act 1994 (TAA). See ¶4-118.

## QUESTIONS WE'VE BEEN ASKED

IR released the following "questions we've been asked" in 2025:

## QB 25/01

QB 25/01, "Income tax — Which rules apply if I rent out my home, part of my home, or a separate dwelling on my property as short-stay accommodation?", sets out how the income tax rules apply if a taxpayer occasionally rents out their home, a room in their home, or a separate dwelling on the property for short stays. See ¶5-232 and ¶10-113.

## QB 25/02

QB 25/02, "Income tax — Which rules apply if I have a dwelling I sometimes rent out as short-stay accommodation and also sometimes use privately?", explains which income tax rules apply to a dwelling that a taxpayer sometimes rents out as short-stay accommodation and also sometimes uses privately. See ¶5-232, ¶10-035 and ¶10-113.

## QB 25/03

QB 25/03, "Income tax — How do the mixed-use asset rules apply if I provide short-stay accommodation?".

This question we've been asked sets out how the mixed-use asset rules apply to a dwelling that a taxpayer sometimes rents out as short-stay accommodation and also sometimes uses privately. See ¶5-232, ¶10-035 and ¶10-113.

QB 25/04

QB 25/04, "Income tax — How do the standard tax rules apply if I provide short-stay accommodation?", explains how the standard tax rules apply to a dwelling that a taxpayer sometimes rents out as short-stay accommodation and also sometimes uses privately. See ¶5-232 and ¶10-113.

## QB 25/05

QB 25/05, "Income tax — If property held in a trust is rented out for short-stay accommodation, who declares the income and what deductions can be claimed?", sets out how the income tax rules apply if property held in a trust is rented out as short-stay accommodation. See ¶5-232 and ¶25-285.

## QB 25/06

QB 25/06, "How does an amalgamated company calculate its available subscribed capital following an amalgamation?", provides detailed guidance on how an amalgamated company calculates its available subscribed capital. See ¶16-760.

## QB 25/07

QB 25/07, "What is the income tax treatment of gift cards and products provided as trade rebates or promotions?", explains the income tax treatment for trade rebate arrangements between businesses where trade suppliers provide products or gift cards to their trade customers. See ¶21-430.

## QB 25/08

QB 25/08, "When is land acquired for a purpose or with an intention of disposal so that the amount derived from the sale is income?", provides guidance on how s CB 6 applies and its relationship with the 2-year bright-line test. See ¶7-045.

## QB 25/09

QB 25/09, "When do I have a "regular pattern" of transactions that prevents me from using exclusions from the land sale rules for my residence or for my main home?" explains when someone is considered to have a "regular pattern" of transactions that prevents them from using the residential exclusion from s CB 6, as well as when someone will have a "regular pattern" of transactions that prevents them from using the main home exclusion from the 2-year bright-line test. See ¶7-211 and ¶7-215.

## QB 25/10

QB 25/10, "On what date is a person treated as acquiring land for the purposes of the land sale rules?", provides guidance on when a person acquires land for the purposes of the land sale rules. See ¶7-047.

## QB 25/11

QB 25/11, "When is the bright-line start date for the 2-year bright-line test?", explains the bright-line start date for the purposes of working out whether residential land is potentially subject to the 2-year bright-line test.

See ¶7-049.

## QB 25/12

QB 25/12, "How does the bright-line test apply to the sale of a subdivided section?", explains how to work out whether a section subdivided from residential land and sold within 2 years can be excluded from the bright-line test. See ¶7-215.

## QB 25/13

QB 25/13, "When is the sale of a lifestyle block excluded from the bright-line test?", explains how to work out whether a lifestyle block sold within 2 years can be excluded from the bright-line test. See ¶7-215.

## QB 25/14

QB 25/14, "When does the business premises exclusion to the bright-line test apply?", explains the business premises exclusion which applies for the purposes of the bright-line test. See ¶7-049.

## QB 25/15

QB 25/15, "How do the bright-line rollover relief provisions apply to transfers of residential land between associated persons?", provides guidance on how the bright-line test and the rollover relief provisions apply to transfers of residential land between associated persons on or after 1 July 2024. See ¶7-545.

## QB 25/16

QB 25/16, "Income tax — How do the income tax rules apply when a close company provides short-stay accommodation?", sets out how the income tax rules apply if property held in a close company is rented out as short-stay accommodation. See ¶5-232 and ¶10-113.

## QB 25/17

QB 25/17, "Income tax: Can I claim a deduction for expenses I incur on repairing a recently acquired capital asset?", considers whether a taxpayer can claim a deduction on the amount of expenditure incurred to repair a capital asset that they have recently acquired. See ¶10-840.

## QB 25/18

QB 25/18, "Does GST apply to a deposit the seller retains in a cancelled land sale agreement", explains the GST implications when a land sale agreement is cancelled and the seller retains the deposit. See ¶32-520.

## QB 25/19

QB 25/19, "GST listed services rules: When is a supply of listed services made through an electronic marketplace?", explains when is a supply made "through" an electronic marketplace. See ¶32-640.

## QB 25/20

QB 25/20, "GST listed services rules: How do the rules apply when there is a supply of listed services and other goods or services?", considers what listed services are and how to apply the GST listed services rules if a supply includes listed services with other goods or services. See ¶32-640.

## QB 25/21

QB 25/21, "Income tax — Public private partnership projects and business continuity test for losses", considers whether a major change in the nature of the contractor's business occurs when the project moves from the design and construction stage to an operational and maintenance phase. See ¶18-035.

## QB 25/22

QB 25/22, "Do the purchase price allocation rules alter the tax book values of Farmland Improvements and listed horticultural plants under subpart DO?", confirms that the purchase price allocation rules do not override the tax treatment of farmland improvements and listed horticultural plants as specified under subpart DO. See ¶33-200.

## DETERMINATIONS

IR released the following determinations in 2025:

## Determination ITR36, "International tax disclosure exemption ITR36" was issued on 31 March 2025

and sets out the disclosure exemption for disclosing FIF and controlled foreign companies (CFC) interests for the 2025 income year. See ¶26-042 and ¶26-110.

## Determination DET 25/01, "GST on supplies through electronic marketplaces — hostel and motel

opt-out agreement criteria" was issued on 14 March 2025 and sets out criteria for when a person who supplies accommodation through an electronic marketplace can enter into an opt-out agreement with the operator of an electronic marketplace. See ¶32-640.

## Determination DET 25/03, "Determination of amount of a particular payment (being per diem

allowances paid in the screen production industry" was issued on 11 June 2025 and applies to per diem allowances paid to contractors/entertainers working in the Screen Production Industry in New Zealand. See ¶3-457.

## 2025 Consumers Price Index (CPI) Adjustment to standard-cost amounts for household services

(childcare, boarding services, or short-stay accommodation). See ¶5-695.

## National Average Market Values of Specified Livestock Determination 2025 (NAMV 2025) which

declared the national average market values for the 2025 income year and applied to specified livestock on hand at the end of the 2024–25 income year. See ¶27-264.

## National Standard Costs for Specified Livestock Determination 2025 (NSC 2025) which declares the

national standard costs for specified livestock for the 2024-25 income year. See ¶27-267.

## OPERATIONAL STATEMENT

IR released the following operational statement in 2025: 2025 CPI adjustment to OS 19/03 (CPI 2025), "Square metre rate for the dual use of premises": containing an update to the square metre rate for the dual use of premises. See ¶10-530. 2025 adjustments to OS 19/04 (KM 2025), "Kilometre rates for the business use of vehicles for the 2025 income year": containing the table of rates for the 2024-25 income year for motor vehicle expenditure claims.

See CCH, Dep Rates 2026, ¶50-084.

OS 25/01, "Cash collateral is "money lent", explains a change in the Commissioner's view that interest arising on cash collateral is subject to RWT or non-resident withholding tax. See ¶10-182.

OS 25/02, "Valuation of livestock", discusses IR's view of the legislation and how taxpayers are expected to deal with certain practical issues from a tax perspective. Specifically, it sets out the options farmers have for valuing the livestock they have on hand at balance date. See ¶27-235.

OS 25/03, "Authority to Act for Tax Agents, Representatives and Nominated Persons: Access to a Client's Inland Revenue Information", sets out how a tax agent, or a representative can obtain the authority to act from their clients and the process for a person to get another person to act for them in relation to their tax affairs and/or their social policy entitlements and obligations. See ¶2-018.

OS 25/04, "The Commissioner of Inland Revenue's search powers", sets out the Commissioner's procedure when exercising their search power under ss 17, 17C and 17D of the TAA and the Search and Surveillance Act 2012. See ¶1-590.

OS 25/05, "Section 17B Notices", sets out the Commissioner's procedure when issuing notices under s 17B of the TAA. See ¶1-590.

## OPERATIONAL POSITION STATEMENT

IR released the following operational position statement in 2025:

## OP 25/01

OP 25/01, "Commissioner's operational position on the GST treatment of fees paid in relation to managed fund", outlines the Commissioner's operational position on the adoption of IS 25/05 (discussed above). See ¶32-044.

## OP 25/02

OP 25/02, "Commissioner's operational position on the FBT treatment of open loop cards provided by employers to employees", outlines the Commissioner's view on the timing of taxpayers' implementation of the position outlined in QB 25/07 (discussed above). See ¶21-430.

## GENERAL ARTICLE

IR released the following general article in 2025: GA 25/01, "Tax on any fees paid to a member of a board, committee, panel, review group or task force", which is prepared specifically in relation to the fees paid to a member of a board, committee, panel, review group or task force under the Cabinet Fees Framework published by the Cabinet Office. See ¶3-280.

## ISSUES PAPER

IR released the following issues papers in 2025:

## "Improving taxation of loans made by companies to shareholders", which discusses IR's concerns

with the current tax rules for taxing shareholder loans and proposes a new time limit rule which would treat certain shareholder loans as dividends if they were not repaid within 12 months from the end of the income year they were made.

## "Income tax treatment of software development expenditure and SaaS customisation and

configuration costs", which discusses the current problems or uncertainties associated with software development costs. Particularly focusing on the following 2 circumstances:

## a software business's costs in developing software that will be sold or licensed to customers

## software configuration or customisation costs incurred by a taxpayer licensed to use a software as a

service (SaaS) application owned by a third party.

## "Thin capitalisation settings for infrastructure", which considers whether New Zealand's thin

capitalisation settings might be discouraging foreign investors from investing in infrastructure projects in New Zealand and proposes 2 possible solutions to address the potential issue.

## "Fringe benefit tax — options for change", which proposes a number of significant changes to the

FBT regime with the aim to simplify the FBT rules and reduce compliance costs for taxpayers.

## "Taxation and the not-for-profit sector", which considers whether certain tax concessions for charities

and not-for-profits continue to be effective and explores whether tax obligations can be simplified and compliance costs minimised.

IR has invited submissions on proposals in the above documents and will use the feedback from the consultations to refine their policy proposals which will then be submitted to the government for consideration.

Last reviewed on 31 December 2025
